\endgroup

[312] The definitions they rest on
{\raggedright The step of the small machine, with the checks it runs and \code{untouched},
minimal/EarnedCore.v. \texttt{prop}, \texttt{eval}, \texttt{fact}, \texttt{fact\_eqb}, \texttt{cost}
and \texttt{run} are as in Definitions~\ref{def:u34-props}, \ref{def:u34-state} and~\ref{def:u34-step}.\par}

[313] The definitions they rest on
In plain words: a core holds the two counters, their versions, the line number, the fact table,
the channel and the trap bit. \code{claim k p c} is the fact made of \texttt{p}, \texttt{c} and
\texttt{c}'s version now. CHECK passes when the trap is down, the checker says yes of the counter's
value, and the table holds fewer than 16 facts, and it adds the claim to the table. COMMIT passes
when the trap is down and that exact claim, version and all, is in the table, and it puts the
claim in the channel in place of whatever was there. CERTIFY takes no operand: it passes when the
trap is down and the channel holds a fact, any fact. A failing one sets the trap and changes nothing
else, and a core with the trap up stays as it is. \code{exec} runs the core's step, adds the
instruction's cost to the ledger, and raises the flag when a passing CERTIFY runs.
\code{untouched s mid c} says each instruction of \texttt{mid}, at the point where it runs from
\texttt{s}, leaves \texttt{c}'s value and version as they were.
Definitions~\ref{def:u34-step} and~\ref{def:u34-untouched} say exactly this.

[314] The definitions they rest on
{\raggedright \code{cg\_uholds}, the meaning of the universal reading checker, with the claims it
reads and the routine's start, coq/kernel/foundation/CompilerChecker.v.\par}

[315] The definitions they rest on
In plain words: a claim is \code{UBase q}, a word of the counter language, or \code{URun r}, a
number naming a reading routine. A word means what it meant on the small machine. A routine claim
holds of a value \texttt{v} when \texttt{r} decodes to a routine (its first line, its code, and
three register numbers and a register count), and the routine, started at its first line with each
register below the count holding the exponent of that register's prime in \texttt{v}, reaches in
some number of steps no larger than the exponent of the fuel register's prime in \texttt{v} a state
outside its code with 1 in its answer register. \code{cg\_ueval\_iff}, printed below, says the
checker's yes is exactly this, and Section~\ref{sec:presented-universal} says the same.

[316] The definitions they rest on
{\raggedright \code{cmpz\_hom}, the simulations Theorem~\ref{thm:parts-nesting} is about, with the
objects they join, coq/kernel/foundation/AxCore.v, AxComplete.v and CzCat.v. \texttt{T.run} is
\texttt{run} of minimal/ThieleComplete.v, printed further down.\par}

[317] The definitions they rest on
{\raggedright \code{small\_chsh\_meaning}, the meaning of the property PCHSH, with the correlators
and the score it's read with, coq/kernel/quantum/SmallChshCheck.v, and the three definitions it
uses from TsirelsonFromAlgebra.v and NPAMomentMatrix.v. \code{npa\_to\_matrix} lays the ten entries
out as the five-by-five matrix $M(E)$ of Section~\ref{sec:chsh}.\par}

[318] The definitions they rest on
{\raggedright The universal base and the interface that carries it, minimal/ThieleComplete.v.
\texttt{machine} has the fields \texttt{m\_state}, \texttt{m\_move}, \texttt{m\_step},
\texttt{m\_cost} and \texttt{m\_record}.\par}

[319] The definitions they rest on
In plain words: a two-counter configuration is a line number and two registers, and
\code{cm\_exec} is what one instruction does to it, the same INC and DEC-or-fall-through as the
small machine's base. A universal base for a machine reads a configuration off each state (its
window), picks out the live states, gives a move for each instruction and a loaded state for each
start; each loaded state shows $(1, a, b)$ and is live, and from every live state the compiled move
changes the window exactly as \code{cm\_exec} says and leaves the state live. The interface lists
its parts and asks nothing of them: the base, the claims, a kind for each move, each claim's
meaning, the checker, ``unchanged,'' the clean states and the ledger. The demands are the four
clauses printed below under certified Thiele-complete. This is part
(a) of Definition~\ref{def:u34-thiele-complete} and the interface it describes.

[320] The universal floor
{\raggedright \code{universal\_nfi\_any\_substrate}, coq/kernel/nfi/UniversalCertificationCost.v, with the record it quantifies over.\par}

[321] The universal floor
In plain words: a Thiele machine is a record of a state type, a move type, a step, a
cost and a yes-or-no reading, plus one more field, \code{cs\_cert\_costs}, which is the toll: a
move that takes the reading from false to true costs at least one. Since the toll is a field,
every system the theorem talks about has it, which is why the same file also states it as
\code{every\_toll\_system\_pays\_the\_floor}. The theorem: start at a state reading false, run
any list of moves (\code{cs\_run} applies them in order, \code{cs\_total\_cost} adds their
costs), end at a state reading true, and the costs add up to at least one.
Observation~\ref{thm:universal-floor} says exactly this; the rule that Definition~\ref{def:certsys}
calls the toll is the record's last field here.

[322] A permanent flip merges
{\raggedright \code{permanent\_flip\_is\_not\_injective}, coq/kernel/nfi/PermanentCertification.v, inside a section that fixes the states, the moves, the step and the reading.\par}

[323] A permanent flip merges
In plain words: \texttt{finite\_states} $\mathit{all}$ says the list $\mathit{all}$ has no repeats and every
state is on it, so there are finitely many. \code{permanent} says once the reading is true,
every move keeps it true. \texttt{step\_injective i} says move $i$ never sends two different
states to the same one. The theorem: on a finite machine with a permanent reading, a move that
takes some state from false to true isn't injective, so it merges two states.
Theorem~\ref{thm:perm-flip-merges} says exactly this, with ``merges'' meaning ``isn't
injective''.

[324] The toll from finiteness, permanence and merge pricing
{\raggedright \code{a2\_from\_merging\_price\_and\_permanence}, the same file and section, with a cost on moves added.\par}

[325] The toll from finiteness, permanence and merge pricing
In plain words: \code{merging\_steps\_priced} says every move that isn't injective costs at
least one, and \code{a2\_holds} is the toll. The theorem: finite, permanent and merges priced
give the toll. Observation~\ref{thm:toll-derived} says this and one thing more, that the machine
with that cost is then a Thiele machine the universal floor applies to. That second half
isn't in this statement. It's the definition right after it in the file
(\code{certification\_system\_from\_merging\_price}), which fills the record's toll field with
this theorem, so the printed form is true, and it leans on two Coq items: this theorem and that
definition.

[326] No exact window price across a collision
{\raggedright \code{shadow\_cannot\_price\_exactly}, coq/kernel/nfi/ShadowPricing.v, inside a section that fixes the states, moves, step, reading and a window \code{obs}.\par}

[327] No exact window price across a collision
In plain words: \texttt{flips s i} says move $i$ takes $s$ from false to true. A shadow price
is any function of what the window shows before and after a step. \code{meets\_floor} says
every flip costs at least one, \code{never\_overcharges} says every step that isn't a flip costs
nothing, and a \code{shadow\_collision} is two steps that look the same through the window,
before and after, one a flip and one not. The theorem: given a collision, no shadow price does
both. Observation~\ref{thm:shadow-price} bundles this with three neighbours from the same file:
that a price meeting the floor charges some non-flip at least one (\code{shadow\_floor\_overcharges}),
that a window showing the reading prices exactly
(\code{window\_showing\_reading\_prices\_exactly}), and that such a window has no collision
(\code{window\_showing\_reading\_has\_no\_collision}). This statement is the second of the four
clauses, and the book says exactly what it says.

[328] The record is a latch
{\raggedright \code{record\_axis\_is\_latch\_holds}, coq/kernel/foundation/StructuralRecordAxis.v; the definitions come from StructuralCoreAnyBase.v and StructuralCoreCover.v.\par}

[329] The record is a latch
In plain words: here a machine $M$ steps by itself (\code{rc\_next} gives the one next
state), carries a reading \code{rc\_cert} and a ledger, and sits over a base $B$ through a
cover \code{C}, a map down to the base's states that commutes with the step.
\code{computation\_driven} says the reading's next value is some function \code{f} of the base
state and the reading's current value. \code{HonestBaseExtension}, the book's base-driven record, means driven, a ledger that never falls, the
toll, a permanent reading, and some reachable step that writes it. \code{latch\_next} is the
latch of an event \code{h}: the base steps, and the record becomes ``already on, or \code{h}
holds now.'' The theorem: every base-driven record evolves exactly as the latch of some event.
Observation~\ref{thm:u11-latch} says a little more than this statement: it names the event,
$h(b) = f(b, \mathrm{off})$, where the statement only says one exists. The proof builds exactly
that event (its first move is \texttt{exists (fun b => f b false)}), so it shows the theorem's
stronger form too. And the proof uses two of the five
conditions, driven and permanent; the other three are carried along and never used. The form
with only those two as hypotheses is \code{record\_axis\_is\_latch\_r1\_r4}
(StructuralClockSync.v), and that is the form Observation~\ref{thm:u11-latch} states.

[330] A raised flag was checked, then committed, then certified
{\raggedright \code{earned\_certification\_same\_claim}, minimal/EarnedCore.v.\par}

[331] A raised flag was checked, then committed, then certified
In plain words: the clean-start premise on \texttt{s0} says the fact table and the channel are
empty and the flag is down, and \texttt{run tr s0} runs a list of instructions. The theorem: from a clean start,
if the flag is up at the end, the list splits as some start, CHECK \texttt{p c}, a middle, COMMIT
of the same \texttt{p c}, another middle, CERTIFY, and the rest; the CHECK, the COMMIT and the
CERTIFY each pass where they run; when the CERTIFY runs, the channel holds the claim the COMMIT
made, which is the fact (\texttt{p}, \texttt{c}, the version \texttt{c} had at the CHECK); the
counter's version at the CHECK equals its version at the COMMIT; and nothing in the first middle
touched that counter. CERTIFY takes no operand, and the channel clause is what ties it to the
claim. Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-cert-earned} says exactly this. The same file keeps the statement
without the channel clause as \code{earned\_certification\_provenance}.

[332] Checker soundness
{\raggedright \code{checker\_soundness}, minimal/EarnedCore.v.\par}

[333] Checker soundness
In plain words: from a clean start, after any list of instructions, every fact in the table
that's about its counter's current version is true of that counter's current value, where
\code{holds} is the property's meaning written without the checker.
Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-soundness} says exactly this in its first sentence; its ``in particular''
is the next corollary in the file, \code{committed\_claim\_holds}.

[334] No forging
{\raggedright \code{no\_forging}, minimal/EarnedCore.v, with the two definitions it uses.\par}

[335] No forging
In plain words: a fact is \code{earned} when a passing CHECK of exactly that property and
counter wrote it somewhere in the list, it is exactly the claim that CHECK made, and, if it's
still about the counter's current version, nothing touched the counter since. \code{no\_forgery}
says every fact in the table at the end is earned. The theorem: from every clean start, every
list of instructions leaves no forgery. That's the last clause of
Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-no-forging}, with Definition~\ref{def:u34-earned} saying the same as
\code{earned}; the two one-step clauses before it are \code{facts\_step} (one instruction at a core) and \code{no\_forging\_step} (a trace with no forgery stays that way when one instruction is added).

[336] The minimum price of a certificate
{\raggedright \code{certified\_run\_min\_cost}, minimal/EarnedCore.v.\par}

[337] The minimum price of a certificate
In plain words: from a clean start, a list of instructions that ends with the flag up cost at
least three in total, and the ledger rose by at least three. Observation~\ref{thm:u34-min-price}
says exactly this in its first sentence; its form for stored programs is
\code{program\_certified\_min\_cost}.

[338] Three, reached by a check that could have failed
{\raggedright \code{earned\_run\_check\_can\_fail} and \code{earned\_run\_refused\_forever}, minimal/EarnedCore.v, with the program they run.\par}

[339] Three, reached by a check that could have failed
In plain words: \code{earned\_run} is CHECK ``$A$ is 0'', COMMIT the same, CERTIFY, and
\texttt{start a b} puts $a$ in $A$ and $b$ in $B$ with an empty table. The first theorem: run it
for four program steps; if $a$ is 0 the flag is up and the ledger is exactly 3, and if $a$
isn't 0 the flag is down and the trap is up. The second: if $a$ isn't 0, the flag is down after
any number of steps. So three is reached by a check that could have failed, and when it fails
nothing ever certifies. Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-tight} says this. The older witness in the same
file, \code{min\_cost\_tight}, reaches three with ``$A$ is at least 0'', a check that can't
fail; the step doesn't police that, and Section~\ref{sec:smallest} says so.

[340] Certified Thiele-complete, the four clauses
{\raggedright \code{thiele\_complete}, minimal/ThieleComplete.v, with the clauses it is made of, and its form over a claim language fixed first, \code{thiele\_complete\_over}. \texttt{load a b} is the interface's loaded state for the start $(1, a, b)$, written inside a section that fixes \texttt{I}; \texttt{run M} runs a list of moves from a state.\par}

[341] Certified Thiele-complete, the four clauses
In plain words: \texttt{M} is a machine (states, moves, step, cost, record) and \texttt{I} an
interface for it (a universal base, claims with meanings, a checker, ``unchanged,'' clean states,
a ledger, and a kind for each move). The first clause says the compiled two-counter moves are
base moves, loaded states are clean, base moves leave the record alone, and the record never comes
down; the universal base's own fields, printed in Section~\ref{sec:coq-definitions}, say the compiled moves act on the window
exactly as the two-counter instructions act. \code{earned\_chain} is a passing check of a claim,
then a commit of the same claim with the thing it's about unchanged at every point between, then
the certify that raises the record. The earned-record clause says clean states have the record
down, every run from a clean state that raises it contains that chain, a passing check means the
claim holds, and a claim that holds keeps holding while its thing is unchanged. The exact toll:
base moves cost 0, record moves 1, and the ledger adds each move's cost. Non-vacuity: some claim's
bare chain raises the record from a loaded state exactly when the claim holds there, and the claim
holds at one loaded state and fails at another. \code{thiele\_complete} asks for one interface
meeting all four. A \code{claim\_language} fixes the claims, an exact equality test on them, their
meanings, ``unchanged,'' and the proof that a meaning keeps holding while its thing is unchanged;
a \code{lang\_interface} over it supplies only the base, the kinds, the checker with its proof of
soundness against the language's meanings, the clean states and the ledger; \code{lang\_ti} makes
it a plain interface whose claims, meanings and ``unchanged'' are the language's; and
\code{thiele\_complete\_over} asks that interface to meet all four.
\texttt{clock} is the two-counter machine with every move costing 1 and any
reading of its configuration as the record. Definition~\ref{def:u34-thiele-complete} says
this, the language-first form included. The file also has a Coq predicate,
\code{weakly\_thiele\_complete} (not printed here), that asks only for the toll and a universal
base. Observation~\ref{thm:u34-clock} says the last line and more: the clock meets
\code{weakly\_thiele\_complete} (\code{clock\_weakly\_thiele\_complete}), a flag that switches on at the first yes
behaves the same way (\code{latch\_clock\_weakly\_thiele\_complete} and
\code{latch\_clock\_not\_thiele\_complete}), and no machine whose every move costs at least 1 is
certified Thiele-complete, which comes from \code{complete\_has\_free\_move}. Clause (c) fixes the unit at
one. Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-tc-unit} states the definition with every record move at one fixed price
$c \ge 1$ (\code{thiele\_complete\_at}, ThieleCompleteScaled.v), shows that class is this one with
its prices multiplied by $c$ (\code{scale\_complete\_at} and \code{unscale\_complete}), and shows the
clock fails at every unit (\code{clock\_not\_thiele\_complete\_at\_any\_unit}).

[342] The universal program's flag and ledger
{\raggedright \code{universal\_halting}, \code{universal\_flag\_iff}, \code{universal\_ledger\_exact} and \code{universal\_thiele\_complete}, coq/kernel/foundation/UniversalRun.v.\par}

[343] The universal program's flag and ledger
In plain words: \texttt{grun P x y m} is $m$ program steps of the small machine's program
$P$ from \texttt{start x y}; \texttt{hrun P x y n} is $n$ program steps of the one fixed host
program \texttt{U} from the host loaded with $P$, $x$ and $y$. The first theorem: the
guest halts at some step exactly when \texttt{U} does. The second: the guest's flag is up at some
step exactly when the host's is. The third: for every guest step count there's a host step count
where the two are related,
with the host count at least the guest count, or both stopped, with equal ledgers; and every host point's ledger lies
between the guest's at some step and at the step after. \texttt{rel} and \texttt{rel\_halt} are the
relations Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-U-sim} spells out. The fourth: the host machine, the small machine
with a register for every whole number and the one property \code{PSlot}, read one move per
instruction, is certified Thiele-complete in the sense of the previous subsection.
Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-U-flag} says exactly the second and the third. Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-U-halting}
says the first and more: at halting the registers, the traps, the ledgers and the flags match too
(\code{universal\_output}, not printed here). Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-U-complete} says the fourth
and more: $U$'s run is a run of the host, the floor on the cost of a run that raises the flag, and the
undecidability of $U$'s halting, each with its own Coq theorem in the table of
Section~\ref{sec:crosswalk}.

[344] The universal reading checker
{\raggedright \code{cg\_run\_check}, \code{cg\_ueval} and \code{cg\_ueval\_iff}, coq/kernel/foundation/CompilerChecker.v.\par}

[345] The universal reading checker
In plain words: a claim is either one of the counter language's words, checked as before, or a
number $r$ naming a reading routine. For the second kind the checker decodes $r$; a code that
names no routine gets false. Otherwise it reads the registers and a fuel number $t$ out of the
value, runs the routine for at most $t$ steps, and says yes exactly when the routine has left its
code with 1 in its answer register. The theorem says the checker's yes is the claim's meaning,
which for a routine is that bounded run. Section~\ref{sec:presented-universal} says exactly this.
The checker answers on every input because decoding terminates and the routine runs for
at most the supplied finite number of steps
(\code{cg\_ueval\_total}).

[346] The surcharge can't always be avoided
{\raggedright \code{presented\_universal\_no\_exact\_below\_three}, coq/kernel/foundation/PresentedUniversal.v.\par}

[347] Nesting pays for every rise, on every host run that follows the guest
{\raggedright \code{cmpz\_hom}, coq/kernel/foundation/CzCat.v, and \code{cmpz\_follows},
\code{cmpz\_follow\_cost\_ge\_exits} and \code{cmpz\_end\_only\_not\_enough},
coq/kernel/foundation/CzFollow.v; \code{cmpz\_follows} and \code{cmpz\_follow\_cost\_ge\_exits}
are written inside a section that fixes \texttt{X}, \texttt{Y} and \texttt{f : cmpz\_hom X Y}.\par}

[348] Nesting pays for every rise, on every host run that follows the guest
In plain words: a morphism $f$ from a guest $X$ to a host $Y$ is a map of states and a monotone
map of records such that the record of an image state is the image of the record, and every move
of the guest is simulated by some list of the host's moves landing on the image of the state
after the move. \code{cmpz\_follows tr s segs} says the host's run is split into one stretch per
guest move, each stretch carrying the image of the state before that move to the image of the
state after it. The first theorem: if the record map reflects the order and $Y$ pays its toll,
then every host run that follows a guest run in that sense costs at least the number of guest
steps whose new record isn't at or below the old one. The second: for host runs that only reach
the image of the guest's end state, no such bound holds in general. On a one-bit machine whose
move $b$ sets the bit to $b$, the guest run ``up, then down'' has one such step, and the empty host
run reaches the image of its end for nothing. Theorem~\ref{thm:parts-nesting}(1) says exactly
these, with ``faithfully'' meaning that $f$ is a morphism, and with \code{cmpz\_follow\_exists} and
\code{cmpz\_follow\_reaches} (CzFollow.v) for the existence of a following run and where it ends.
The earlier form, \code{cmpz\_sim\_cost\_ge\_exits} (CzCat.v), finds one host run reaching the end
image at that cost. It bounds the host's cost by the guest's rises. It says nothing about the
guest's other charges.

[349] The copy is the record, on the simpler host
{\raggedright \code{record\_agreement}, minimal/UniversalThiele.v.\par}

[350] The copy is the record, on the simpler host
In plain words: the host keeps a mirror bit \code{mrec} beside the guest's state \code{gst},
and \texttt{agrees h} says the mirror equals the guest's own flag. The theorem: a host state that
agrees still agrees after any list of host moves, whatever they are.
Observation~\ref{thm:u34-mirror} says this for lists of moves and adds single moves, stored programs, loading,
and the mirror after $n$ moves GSTEP 1,
which are \code{record\_agreement\_step}, \code{record\_agreement\_program}, \code{hload\_agrees} and \code{simulated\_record}.

[351] No free host certification, on the simpler host
{\raggedright \code{no\_free\_host\_certification}, minimal/UniversalThiele.v, with the host's moves and their costs.\par}

[352] No free host certification, on the simpler host
In plain words: a host move is either \texttt{OWN i}, one of the small machine's own
instructions on the host's core, or \texttt{GSTEP b}, one guest step with budget \code{b}, which
costs \code{b} whatever the guest instruction cost. \texttt{crossing g g'} says the guest's flag
went from down to up. The theorem: if the mirror is down before a list of host moves and up
after it, then somewhere in the list there's a \texttt{GSTEP b} with \code{b} at least one at
which the guest's flag crossed. Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-no-free-host} prints the one-move form,
\code{no\_free\_host\_certification\_step}, which also says the host wasn't trapped and the
guest's next instruction was CERTIFY; this is the form for whole lists that it lifts to. The printed theorem says two things more, each with its own Coq theorem: a trace of OWN moves alone leaves the mirror and
the guest state where they were (\code{guestless\_runs\_leave\_mirror}), and the host's own flag rises only by an OWN CERTIFY that passes (\code{own\_record\_only\_by\_certify}). Neither the one-move form nor the list form
says what the guest did before its CERTIFY. That, after a run from a loaded host, a raised mirror means
the guest's own run
checked and committed first is the next theorem.

[353] A raised mirror stands on the guest's own earned chain, on the simpler host
{\raggedright \code{host\_mirror\_earned}, minimal/UniversalThiele.v.\par}

[354] A raised mirror stands on the guest's own earned chain, on the simpler host
In plain words: load the host with a guest program $P$ and a guest start \texttt{(x, y)}
(\code{hload} gives the guest a clean start), run any list of host moves, and suppose the
mirror is up at the end. Then for some number of steps \code{n}, the guest's state is its own
program's run of \code{n} steps, and the instructions that run executed split exactly the way
Theorem~\ref{thm:u34-cert-earned} splits a trace: a passing CHECK, a COMMIT of the same claim at
the same version with the counter untouched between, then a passing CERTIFY. So the host's
mirror can't be up unless the guest's own program earned it. The book's sentences citing
\code{host\_mirror\_earned} say this in words; the Coq says a little more than they need, since the chain
sits inside the instructions the guest program itself executed. Any list containing the chain
would have been enough for them.

[355] Structural entitlement, represented
{\raggedright \code{ent\_representation}, minimal/EntitlementSmall.v, with the definitions it leans on.\par}

[356] Structural entitlement, represented
In plain words: take a machine and an interface through which it is certified Thiele-complete, any type of
rivals $X$ and of observations $O$ with an exact equality test, a run $t$ from a clean
state $s_0$, and a decoder $d$ that reads the run's receipts, its list of moves. Hypotheses
in order: the posterior list is inside the prior and drops at least one rival; some dropped rival
shows an observation no survivor shows; the start is clean; the run ends with the record up and the
posterior's membership test accepts the decoded run; the tree is no deeper than the number of record
moves in the run; the posterior is non-empty; and the covering is by fibres, as
\code{ent\_reduction} spells out. Conclusions in order: the posterior's test is strictly stronger; the
run contains the earned chain; some CHECK of a claim and a later COMMIT of the same claim appear in the run, with the claim holding at both; the index
bits are at most the record moves; the record moves are exactly the ledger's rise; and the rise is at
least 3. Theorem~\ref{thm:ent-machine} says this, with the hypotheses numbered (M1) to (M6)
and the conclusions numbered (1) to (5), and it says one thing more in (2): there the meanings hold at the chain's own CHECK and COMMIT. The Coq's third conclusion is a separate existential that doesn't name
the chain; the printed form follows from it with the last two parts of clause (b), a passing check means the claim holds and an unchanged thing keeps it holding.

[357] Structural entitlement, represented
{\raggedright And its form with the narrowing made by the run, \code{ent\_run\_entitlement}, same file, with the definitions it leans on.\par}

[358] Structural entitlement, represented
In plain words: \code{ent\_passes} walks the run and keeps, for each CHECK that passes at the state
the run is in, the moves before it and its claim; \code{ent\_run\_tests} turns each into a test of
a candidate start $x$, replay those moves from $x$ and check the same claim; the posterior keeps
the candidates that pass every test, in order; and \code{ent\_stages} records the number of
candidates standing before and after each test. Hypotheses: the machine is certified Thiele-complete through
\texttt{I}, the start is clean, the run ends with the record up, the start is among the
candidates, and every test keeps at least a $2^{-k}$ share. Conclusions in order: the posterior is
inside the prior and keeps the start; every dropped candidate fails, replayed, a CHECK the run made
and passed; the exact product count; the index bits are at most $k$ times the tests passed; the
tests passed are at most the record moves; the record moves are the ledger's rise; the index bits
are at most $k$ times that rise; the earned chain; and a rise of at least 3.
Theorem~\ref{thm:ent-run-machine} (1) to (5) says exactly this; its part (6) is
\code{ent\_run\_round\_bits}, not printed here.

[359] A raised flag stands on the CHSH check
{\raggedright \code{small\_chsh\_flag\_implies\_tsirelson}, coq/kernel/quantum/SmallChshMachine.v, over the host machine with the one property \code{small\_chsh\_PCHSH}.\par}

[360] A raised flag stands on the CHSH check
In plain words: start from a clean start and run any list of instructions of the host machine whose
only property is the CHSH one, and suppose the flag is up at the end. Then the list splits as some
instructions, a CHECK of the property at some counter $c$, some more, a COMMIT of the same
property at the same counter, some more, a CERTIFY, and the rest. Take the value $v$ that
counter $c$ held just before the CHECK, and read it as a tally of eight counts. That tally
passes the seven integer facts (\code{small\_chsh\_check}); it means what the check is for: every
question pair sampled and the pinned five-by-five symmetric and positive semidefinite
(\code{small\_chsh\_meaning}); its score $S$ has $S^2 \le 8$ and $|S| \le 2\sqrt 2$; and counter
$c$ still held $v$ when the COMMIT ran. Because that tally passes the check, its score is in fact
strictly below the bound, $S^2 < 8$ (\code{nec\_e\_check\_strict}), and passing tallies come as
close to 8 as you like (\code{nec\_e\_check\_sharp}), both in NecEChsh.v. Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-earned} says exactly this in
its first part. It says more in two places that have their own Coq theorems, the stored-program form
and the converse that the three instructions certify exactly when the check passes.

[361] The recursion theorem up to the record, but not on every register
{\raggedright \code{sm2\_kleene\_obs} and \code{sm2\_no\_exact}, coq/kernel/foundation/SmFixedPoint.v and coq/kernel/foundation/SmNoExact.v, with the relation they use. Here \code{M} is the host machine's module, \code{hends P x s} says program \code{P}, started with $x$ in register 1 and everything else clean, stops (a trap counts as stopping) in state \code{s}, and \code{sm\_computes\_map T F} says that on the number of every program \code{p}, \code{T} stops with the number of \code{F p}.\par}

[362] The recursion theorem up to the record, but not on every register
In plain words: \code{sm2\_obs s t} says two final states agree on register 0, the trap latch, the
ledger, the flag, the number of facts and whether the channel is empty, and nothing else.
\code{sm2\_obs\_equiv P Q} says that on every input, any state \code{P} ends in has a state \code{Q}
ends in that agrees in that sense, and the same the other way round, which includes that \code{P}
stops on an input exactly when \code{Q} does. The first theorem: for every transformation $F$
of host programs that some host program \code{T} carries out on numbers, there is a program \code{e}
that agrees with \code{F e} in that sense. \code{T} is any host program, and may itself check, commit
and certify. The second: there is a transformation $F$, carried out by a host program, such that
no program \code{e} behaves the same as \code{F e} in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:host-same}, which
compares every register, the fact table, the channel, the trap latch, the ledger and the flag.
Theorems~\ref{thm:host-kleene-record} and~\ref{thm:host-no-exact} say these two. The book's printed
version says exactly this, with the line number and versions left out of both comparisons, as the
definition does.

[363] Setting and notation
The same polynomial $S$ appears under three names in the source: \code{CHSH}
(kernel/quantum/TsirelsonGeneral.v), \code{CHSH\_value} (kernel/quantum/TsirelsonFromAlgebra.v)
and \code{chsh\_S} (kernel/quantum/ElliptopeCompletion.v).

[364] Setting and notation
\begin{definition}[Counters]\label{def:chsh-counters}
Eight counts sit in the record \code{WitnessCounts}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v): for each setting pair, $\mathrm{same}_{xy}$ counts rounds
whose two answer bits matched and $\mathrm{diff}_{xy}$ counts rounds whose bits differed. A
round adds one to exactly one of the eight. Write
\[
N_{xy} = \mathrm{same}_{xy} + \mathrm{diff}_{xy}, \qquad
D_{xy} = \mathrm{same}_{xy} - \mathrm{diff}_{xy}, \qquad
E_{xy} = \frac{D_{xy}}{N_{xy}} .
\]
\end{definition}

[365] Setting and notation
A matched round contributes $ab = +1$ and a differing round $ab = -1$, so $D_{xy}$ is the sum
of the products and $D_{xy}/N_{xy}$ is their average. Since $|D_{xy}| \le N_{xy}$, every
correlator from counters lies in $[-1, 1]$. In Coq the integers are \code{chsh\_n\_z} and \code{chsh\_d\_z}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v). When a bucket is empty, the real-valued correlator
\code{state\_bucket\_correlation} (kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v) and the rational one
\code{chsh\_correlator\_q} (kernel/quantum/CHSHStatisticalBridge.v) both return 0 by
convention. The CHSH score of a tally, \code{chsh\_stat\_from\_wc} (CHSHStatisticalBridge.v),
adds the first three correlators and obtains $-E_{11}$ by swapping the two counters of the
last bucket.

[366] The classical bound
The Coq proofs do not factor; they split into the sixteen sign cases and compute. The
rational statement is \code{local\_S\_2\_deterministic} (kernel/quantum/BoxCHSH.v): for
$f_A, f_B : \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{Q}$ with every value $\pm 1$, the absolute value of
$f_A(0) f_B(0) + f_A(0) f_B(1) + f_A(1) f_B(0) - f_A(1) f_B(1)$ is at most 2. The real
statement is \code{deterministic\_strategy\_chsh\_bounded} (kernel/quantum/MinorConstraints.v),
with conclusion $-2 \le \cdots \le 2$.

[367] The classical bound
\begin{definition}[Finite factorizable model, \code{is\_factorizable}]\label{def:chsh-factorizable}
A real-valued correlation function $E(a, b, x, y)$ is factorizable
(kernel/quantum/MinorConstraints.v) when there exist response functions $A(x, \lambda)$,
$B(y, \lambda)$, weights $p(\lambda)$ and a natural number $\lambda_{\max}$ such that:
$0 \le p(\lambda) \le 1$ for $\lambda \le \lambda_{\max}$;
$\sum_{\lambda = 0}^{\lambda_{\max}} p(\lambda) = 1$; every $A(x, \lambda)$ and every
$B(y, \lambda)$ is $-1$ or $+1$; and for all $a, b, x, y$,
$E(a, b, x, y) = \sum_{\lambda=0}^{\lambda_{\max}} p(\lambda) A(x, \lambda) B(y, \lambda)$.
The outcome arguments $a, b$ are dummies; only correlations are modelled.
\end{definition}

[368] The classical bound
\begin{theorem}[\code{fine\_theorem}, one direction only]\label{thm:chsh-fine}
If $E$ is factorizable, then $-2 \le E(0,0,0,0) + E(0,0,0,1) + E(0,0,1,0) - E(0,0,1,1) \le 2$
(kernel/quantum/MinorConstraints.v; restated as \code{factorizable\_CHSH\_classical\_bound}).
\end{theorem}

[369] The classical bound
\begin{remark}[What the name does and does not cover]\label{rem:chsh-fine-scope}
Fine's 1982 theorem is an equivalence between several descriptions of local models and the
Bell inequalities. \code{fine\_theorem} proves one direction: a finite factorizable model obeys
the bound. Its converse, as it's stated, is false (\code{nec\_e\_fine\_converse\_false}); the
equivalence in the form Fine meant, for correlations, with each correlator between $-1$ and $1$ and all eight inequalities, is
\code{nec\_e\_fine\_iff}. And \code{fine\_theorem} covers only finitely many hidden states
($\lambda \le \lambda_{\max}$). An arbitrary probability space is outside it. The transfer to rational
boxes is \code{local\_box\_CHSH\_bound} (kernel/quantum/MinorConstraints.v): if
\textnormal{\texttt{box\_correlations B}} is factorizable then the real image of $|S(B)|$ is at most 2.
\end{remark}

[370] The classical bound
\begin{definition}[\code{WCLocallyConsistent}]\label{def:chsh-wclc}
For bits $a_0, a_1, b_0, b_1 \in \{0, 1\}$ and a counter record $w$
(kernel/quantum/CHSHStatisticalBridge.v), $w$ is locally consistent with
$(a_0, a_1, b_0, b_1)$ when, for each setting pair $(x, y)$: if $a_x = b_y$ then
$\mathrm{diff}_{xy} = 0$, otherwise $\mathrm{same}_{xy} = 0$; and every bucket is sampled,
$N_{00}, N_{01}, N_{10}, N_{11} > 0$.
\end{definition}

[371] The classical bound
\begin{theorem}[A tally above 2 is not one replayed strategy]\label{thm:chsh-tally}
Let $w$ be a counter record whose rational score \code{chsh\_stat\_from\_wc} $w$ is greater than 2.
Then for all bits $a_0, a_1, b_0, b_1$, $w$ is not locally consistent with
$(a_0, a_1, b_0, b_1)$. This is \code{chsh\_violation\_rules\_out\_locally\_factorizable\_coupling}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHCouplingBridge.v). A stronger companion,
\code{locally\_consistent\_classical\_bound}, states that a locally consistent record has
score of absolute value at most 2; read through its contrapositive, it gives the first statement.
\end{theorem}

[372] Tsirelson's bound from algebra
\begin{lemma}[The six-square identity, \code{chsh\_gap\_is\_sum\_of\_squares}]\label{lem:chsh-sos}
For all real $a, b, c, d$,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&4(a^2 + b^2 + c^2 + d^2) - (a + b + c - d)^2 \\
&\quad = (a-b)^2 + (a-c)^2 + (a+d)^2 + (b-c)^2 + (b+d)^2 + (c+d)^2 .
\end{aligned}
\]
(kernel/quantum/TsirelsonFromAlgebra.v, closed by \code{ring}.)
\end{lemma}

[373] Tsirelson's bound from algebra
Since the right side is a sum of squares, $S^2 \le 4 \sum_{xy} E_{xy}^2$ for any four reals;
this is \code{cauchy\_schwarz\_chsh} (TsirelsonFromAlgebra.v).

[374] Tsirelson's bound from algebra
\begin{theorem}[Row-bound form of Tsirelson's bound]\label{thm:chsh-row-tsirelson}
For all real $E_{00}, E_{01}, E_{10}, E_{11}$ with $E_{00}^2 + E_{01}^2 \le 1$ and
$E_{10}^2 + E_{11}^2 \le 1$, $S^2 \le 8$. This is \code{tsirelson\_squared}
(kernel/quantum/TsirelsonFromAlgebra.v) and \code{tsirelson\_from\_row\_bounds}
(kernel/quantum/TsirelsonGeneral.v). The absolute form $|S| \le \sqrt 8 = 2\sqrt 2$ is
\code{tsirelson\_bound\_abs} (TsirelsonGeneral.v).
\end{theorem}

[375] Tsirelson's bound from algebra
\begin{theorem}[Tightness]\label{thm:chsh-tight}
The point $E_{00} = E_{01} = E_{10} = 1/\sqrt 2$, $E_{11} = -1/\sqrt 2$ satisfies both row
bounds and has $S = \sqrt 8$. This is \code{tsirelson\_tight} (TsirelsonFromAlgebra.v) and
\code{tsirelson\_achievable} (TsirelsonGeneral.v).
\end{theorem}

[376] Tsirelson's bound from algebra
\begin{remark}[Where equality can happen]\label{rem:chsh-equality}
The step about where equality happens is checked as \code{nec\_e\_equality\_forces\_point} and
\code{nec\_e\_equality\_two\_points}, and the fact that no ratio of whole numbers solves $2a^2 = 1$
as \code{nec\_e\_no\_rational\_half\_square} (NecEChshEquality.v); the conclusion about
tallies at the end is checked as \code{nec\_e\_no\_sqrt8}, \code{nec\_e\_check\_strict},
\code{nec\_e\_check\_never\_tsirelson} and \code{nec\_e\_check\_sharp} (NecEChsh.v). Equality $S^2 = 8$ under the row bounds forces
equality in both steps of the proof above: the six squares must all vanish, which gives
$a = b = c = -d$, and the rows must be tight, which gives $2a^2 = 1$. So the point of
Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-tight} and its negative are the only places the bound is reached. A tally's correlators are
ratios of whole numbers and $2a^2 = 1$ has no such solution, so no tally reaches the bound; the integer
check's passing tallies stay strictly below it and come as near as you like.
\end{remark}

[377] Tsirelson's bound from algebra
\begin{lemma}[A rational constant above the bound, \code{rational\_tsirelson\_bound}]\label{lem:chsh-rational}
$\sqrt 8 < 5657/2000$ (kernel/quantum/TsirelsonFromAlgebra.v).
\end{lemma}

[378] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{definition}[The pinned level-1 matrix]\label{def:chsh-five}
\code{zero\_marginal\_npa} (kernel/quantum/NPAMomentMatrix.v) fills the general record
\code{NPAMomentMatrix} with all four one-party entries and both within-party entries set
to zero, and \code{npa\_to\_matrix} lays it out on rows and columns
$I, A_0, A_1, B_0, B_1$:
\end{definition}

[379] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
Eigenvalues never come into the source's definition of positive semidefiniteness. It is defined
by quadratic forms: \code{PSD5} (kernel/quantum/ConstructivePSD.v) says $v^{\mathsf T} M v \ge 0$ for every
$v \in \mathbb{R}^5$, and \code{npa\_psd} (NPAMomentMatrix.v) is ``symmetric and \code{PSD5}''.
In block form,

[380] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
The lemma is checked in general form, for finite index sets and any symmetric $P$ with a symmetric
inverse, as \code{schur\_identity} and \code{schur\_complement\_psd} (SchurComplement.v); the
forward direction needs no positivity at all, and the converse needs $P$ positive semidefinite.
The proofs of the checks don't invoke it abstractly. They carry out its one instance with
$P = I_2$, $Q = E$, $R = I_2$, where the complement is
\[
I_2 - E^{\mathsf T} E =
\begin{pmatrix}
1 - E_{00}^2 - E_{10}^2 & -(E_{00} E_{01} + E_{10} E_{11}) \\
-(E_{00} E_{01} + E_{10} E_{11}) & 1 - E_{01}^2 - E_{11}^2
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
The diagonal of $E^{\mathsf T} E$ holds the squared lengths of the columns of $E$, which is
why the conditions below are column conditions.

[381] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{definition}[Column contractive, \code{zero\_marginal\_column\_contractive}]\label{def:chsh-cc}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v) Write
$p = 1 - E_{00}^2 - E_{10}^2$, $q = 1 - E_{01}^2 - E_{11}^2$ and
$s = E_{00} E_{01} + E_{10} E_{11}$. The correlators are column contractive when
\[
p \ge 0, \qquad q \ge 0, \qquad p\,q - s^2 \ge 0 .
\]
\end{definition}

[382] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{lemma}[The 2x2 test, \code{psd2\_quadratic\_form\_nonneg}]\label{lem:chsh-psd2}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v) If $\alpha \ge 0$, $\delta \ge 0$ and
$\alpha\delta - \beta^2 \ge 0$, then $\alpha u^2 + 2\beta u w + \delta w^2 \ge 0$ for all real
$u, w$.
\end{lemma}

[383] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{theorem}[Contractive implies PSD]\label{thm:chsh-cc-psd}
If $p \ge 0$, $q \ge 0$ and $pq - s^2 \ge 0$, then \code{PSD5} holds of $M(E)$.
This is \code{zero\_marginal\_npa\_column\_contractive\_implies\_psd}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v).
\end{theorem}

[384] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{lemma}[Discriminant, \code{quadratic\_nonneg\_discriminant}]\label{lem:chsh-discriminant}
(kernel/quantum/ConstructivePSD.v) If $a + 2bt + ct^2 \ge 0$ for every real $t$, then
$b^2 \le ac$.
\end{lemma}

[385] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{theorem}[PSD implies contractive]\label{thm:chsh-psd-cc}
If \code{PSD5} holds of $M(E)$, then $p \ge 0$, $q \ge 0$ and $pq - s^2 \ge 0$. This is
\code{npa\_psd\_implies\_column\_contractive} (kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v).
\end{theorem}

[386] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{proof}
Feed the quadratic form three test vectors, written in the order
$(I, A_0, A_1, B_0, B_1)$; their values are the lemmas \code{npa\_quad5\_test\_col0},
\code{npa\_quad5\_test\_col1} and \code{npa\_quad5\_test\_schur} (CHSHColumnCheck.v).
Each is the choice $x = -E y$ that zeroes both completed squares in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-cc-psd}, so only $y^{\mathsf T}(I_2 - E^{\mathsf T} E) y$ is left.

[387] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{corollary}[The equivalence]\label{cor:chsh-iff}
For all real $E_{00}, E_{01}, E_{10}, E_{11}$, \code{PSD5} of $M(E)$ holds if and only if the
correlators are column contractive: \code{npa\_psd\_iff\_column\_contractive}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v). With symmetry packaged in,
\code{column\_contractive\_iff\_npa\_psd} states contractivity if and only if \code{npa\_psd} holds
of \code{zero\_marginal\_npa} at the four correlators.
\end{corollary}

[388] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{theorem}[PSD implies Tsirelson]\label{thm:chsh-psd-tsirelson}
If \code{npa\_psd} holds of \code{zero\_marginal\_npa} at $E_{00}, E_{01}, E_{10}, E_{11}$, then $S^2 \le 8$, and
$|S| \le \sqrt 8$. These are \code{npa\_psd\_implies\_tsirelson\_bound} and
\code{npa\_psd\_implies\_tsirelson\_bound\_abs}.
\end{theorem}

[389] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{proof}
\code{psd\_3x3\_determinant\_nonneg} (kernel/quantum/ConstructivePSD.v) says a symmetric PSD
matrix with unit diagonal has every $3 \times 3$ principal minor
$1 - x^2 - y^2 - z^2 + 2xyz \ge 0$. On rows $A_0, B_0, B_1$ the entries are
$x = E_{00}$, $y = E_{01}$, $z = 0$, so $E_{00}^2 + E_{01}^2 \le 1$. On rows $A_1, B_0, B_1$
the same gives $E_{10}^2 + E_{11}^2 \le 1$. This is
\code{npa\_psd\_zero\_marginal\_implies\_row\_bounds}. Then
Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-row-tsirelson} applies, through \code{tsirelson\_from\_minors}
(kernel/quantum/TsirelsonGeneral.v).
\end{proof}

[390] The zero-marginal 5x5 moment matrix
\begin{remark}[What PSD of the pinned matrix means]\label{rem:chsh-scope-five}
$M(E)$ is the level-1 moment matrix with the two within-party entries $\langle A_0 A_1
\rangle$ and $\langle B_0 B_1 \rangle$ pinned to zero. Its PSD is sufficient for the
correlators to admit some PSD completion (item G, \code{zero\_marginal\_implies\_elliptope}), and it
is not necessary: the all-ones plan has a completion but fails here. So a check built on
$M(E)$ is sound for the completion set and not complete for it, and it rejects some honest
classical tallies. That correlators with a PSD completion are exactly those reachable by
quantum measurements is Tsirelson's theorem, and both halves are proved here in finite dimension.
For real and for complex amplitudes, a strategy's correlators are inner products of unit vectors
(\code{qs\_corr\_dot}, \code{qs\_uvec\_unit} and \code{qs\_vvec\_unit}; with complex amplitudes,
\code{qc\_corr\_dot}, \code{qc\_uvec\_unit} and \code{qc\_vvec\_unit}, the vectors read as real
ones of twice the length) and its level-1 moment matrix is PSD (\code{qs\_npa\_psd},
\code{qc\_npa\_psd}), so they have a PSD completion (\code{tr\_quantum\_elliptope},
\code{tr\_complex\_elliptope}). The other half, that every PSD completion comes from a quantum
strategy, is \code{tr\_representation}: the strategy is built on two eight-level systems from
the completion's Gram vectors (item H).
\end{remark}

[391] The integer check
\begin{definition}[\code{column\_contractive\_check\_witness}]\label{def:chsh-check}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v) From the counters of Definition~\ref{def:chsh-counters}, with all
arithmetic in $\mathbb{Z}$, let
\[
\begin{aligned}
A &= N_{00}^2 N_{10}^2 - D_{00}^2 N_{10}^2 - D_{10}^2 N_{00}^2, \\
B &= N_{01}^2 N_{11}^2 - D_{01}^2 N_{11}^2 - D_{11}^2 N_{01}^2, \\
C &= D_{00} D_{01} N_{10} N_{11} + D_{10} D_{11} N_{00} N_{01}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The check is the conjunction of seven facts, in this order:
$0 < N_{00}$, $0 < N_{01}$, $0 < N_{10}$, $0 < N_{11}$, $0 \le A$, $0 \le B$, and
$C \cdot C \le A \cdot B$.
\end{definition}

[392] The integer check
\begin{theorem}[Soundness of the integer check]\label{thm:chsh-check-sound}
If \code{column\_contractive\_check\_witness} $w$ = \code{true}, then the four correlators
\code{state\_bucket\_correlation} $\mathrm{same}_{xy}\ \mathrm{diff}_{xy}$ of $w$ are column
contractive. This is \code{column\_contractive\_check\_witness\_sound}
(kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v), and \code{column\_contractive\_check\_witness\_npa\_psd}
(same file) carries it on to PSD of the pinned matrix.
\end{theorem}

[393] The integer check
\begin{corollary}[A passed check implies the Tsirelson bound]\label{cor:chsh-check-bound}
If \code{column\_contractive\_check\_witness} $w$ = \code{true}, the correlators of $w$ satisfy
$S^2 \le 8$. It is the composition of Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-check-sound},
Corollary~\ref{cor:chsh-iff} and Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-psd-tsirelson}.
\end{corollary}
On the small machine the same conclusion is one checked theorem,
\code{small\_chsh\_check\_tsirelson} (SmallChshCheck.v), and its earned form
is \code{small\_chsh\_flag\_implies\_tsirelson} (SmallChshMachine.v); see Section~\ref{sec:chsh}.

[394] Level 1+AB
The source's comments index Alice's observables $A_1, A_2$ and Bob's $B_1, B_2$, so its
$\langle A_1 B_1\rangle$ is $E_{00}$ here. The Coq variables themselves are already named in the book's order, from $e_{00}$ to $e_{11}$. The five extra
parameters are named in the record \code{Q1ABMoments} (kernel/quantum/QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v):
$\gamma_1$ for $\langle A_0 A_1 B_0 \rangle$, $\gamma_2$ for $\langle A_0 A_1 B_1 \rangle$,
$\gamma_3$ for $\langle A_0 B_0 B_1 \rangle$, $\gamma_4$ for $\langle A_1 B_0 B_1 \rangle$,
$\gamma_5$ for $\langle A_0 A_1 B_0 B_1 \rangle$. These are labels only; the file builds no
operators.

[395] Level 1+AB
\begin{definition}[\code{q1ab\_moment\_matrix}]\label{def:chsh-nine}
(kernel/quantum/QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v) The $9 \times 9$ real matrix on rows and
columns $I, A_0, A_1, B_0, B_1, A_0B_0, A_0B_1, A_1B_0, A_1B_1$, with the entries exactly as
the Coq definition places them:
\end{definition}

[396] Level 1+AB
Two constraints are fixed by this layout. First, the four-body cells carry opposite signs:
$+\gamma_5$ at $(A_0B_0, A_1B_1)$ and $-\gamma_5$ at $(A_0B_1, A_1B_0)$, guarded by
\code{q1ab\_four\_body\_cells\_are\_conjugate} (QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v). In a real
moment matrix those two cells are $\langle A_0 A_1 B_0 B_1 \rangle$ and $\langle A_0 A_1 B_1 B_0
\rangle$, so the tie says $\langle A_0 A_1 (B_0 B_1 + B_1 B_0) \rangle = 0$, which for a real
matrix is $\langle \{A_0, A_1\} \otimes \{B_0, B_1\} \rangle = 0$. The level-1 pinnings don't
imply it. Take a classical strategy: Alice's two answers $a_0, a_1$ are independent fair signs,
and Bob copies them, $b_0 = a_0$ and $b_1 = a_1$. Every pinned cell of its moment matrix is 0 and
both four-body cells are 1, so no choice of the nine numbers gives its matrix
(\code{q1ab\_tie\_is\_a\_constraint}, QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v). The matrix here is
therefore level 1+AB on the slice $\langle \{A_0, A_1\} \otimes \{B_0, B_1\} \rangle = 0$, and that
is what ``level 1+AB'' names in this book. The slice holds whenever $A_0$ and $A_1$ anticommute.
The soundness theorems below are about this matrix, so they stand as stated. Second, the matrix
keeps the level-1 pinning: every cell whose label reduces to a marginal or to
$\langle A_0 A_1 \rangle$ or $\langle B_0 B_1 \rangle$ is zero, for instance
$(A_0, A_0B_0)$, $(A_0B_0, A_0B_1)$ and $(A_0B_0, A_1B_0)$. Symmetry is
\code{q1ab\_moment\_matrix\_symmetric}.

[397] Level 1+AB
\begin{definition}[The residual, \code{q1ab\_residual}]\label{def:chsh-residual}
(QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v)
\[
\begin{aligned}
R ={}& v_{B_0}^2 + v_{B_1}^2 + v_{00}^2 + v_{01}^2 + v_{10}^2 + v_{11}^2 \\
&+ 2 v_{B_0} (\gamma_3 v_{01} + \gamma_4 v_{11}) + 2 v_{B_1} (\gamma_3 v_{00} + \gamma_4 v_{10}) \\
&+ 2 \gamma_5 (v_{00} v_{11} - v_{01} v_{10}) - L_1^2 - L_2^2 - L_3^2 .
\end{aligned}
\]
The predicate \code{column\_contractive\_q1ab} says $R \ge 0$ for all six real inputs.
\end{definition}

[398] Level 1+AB
\begin{lemma}[Three squares plus the residual, \code{quad9\_q1ab\_sos\_decomposition}]\label{lem:chsh-nine-sos}
(QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v, closed by \code{ring}.) For every vector,
\[
v^{\mathsf T} M_9\, v = (v_I + L_1)^2 + (v_{A_0} + L_2)^2 + (v_{A_1} + L_3)^2 + R .
\]
\end{lemma}

[399] Level 1+AB
\begin{theorem}[PSD at level 1+AB, \code{q1ab\_psd\_iff\_column\_contractive}]\label{thm:chsh-nine-iff}
(QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v) For all real $E_{xy}$ and $\gamma_1, \ldots, \gamma_5$,
\code{PSD9} of the matrix of Definition~\ref{def:chsh-nine} holds if and only if \code{column\_contractive\_q1ab}
holds. The directions are \code{column\_contractive\_q1ab\_implies\_psd9} and
\code{psd9\_implies\_column\_contractive\_q1ab}.
\end{theorem}

[400] Level 1+AB
As a quadratic form in $(v_{B_0}, v_{B_1}, v_{00}, v_{01}, v_{10}, v_{11})$, numbered 1 to 6,
the residual has the symmetric coefficient matrix $H$ with these upper entries. They are the
\code{q12345\_H11} to \code{q12345\_H66} definitions (QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v), and
\code{q12345\_sym6\_qf\_equals\_residual} proves the form equals $R$. Each entry can be read
back off $R$ by collecting the coefficient of $v_i v_j$.

[401] Level 1+AB
\begin{lemma}[Fraction-free Schur step]\label{lem:chsh-schur-step}
For a symmetric $n \times n$ matrix $h$ and any vector $v$, with
$S_{ij} = h_{11} h_{ij} - h_{1i} h_{1j}$ for $i, j \ge 2$ (here $S$ is the Schur matrix;
the score plays no part
in this lemma),
\[
h_{11} \, v^{\mathsf T} h\, v = \Bigl(\sum_{j} h_{1j} v_j\Bigr)^2 + \sum_{i,j \ge 2} S_{ij} v_i v_j .
\]
So if $h_{11} > 0$ and the $(n-1) \times (n-1)$ matrix $S$ gives a nonnegative form, then $h$
does too. The instances used are \code{sym6\_Schur\_identity} (QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v) and
\code{sym5\_Schur\_identity}, with the conclusions \code{sym6\_qf\_nonneg\_from\_pd} and
\code{sym5\_qf\_nonneg\_from\_pd}.
\end{lemma}

[402] Level 1+AB
\begin{lemma}[Sylvester for 4x4, \code{sym4\_qf\_nonneg\_from\_pd}]\label{lem:chsh-sylvester}
(QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v) Let $h$ be symmetric $4 \times 4$ with leading principal
minors $d_1, d_2, d_3, d_4$ (\code{sym4\_d1} to \code{sym4\_d4}). If all four are
strictly positive, then $v^{\mathsf T} h\, v \ge 0$ for all $v$.
\end{lemma}

[403] Level 1+AB
\begin{proof}
\code{sym4\_LDLT\_identity} (closed by \code{ring}) is the fraction-free factorization
\[
d_1^2 d_2 d_3 \, v^{\mathsf T} h\, v = d_1 d_2 d_3 \, P_1^2 + d_1 d_3 \, Q_1^2 + Q_2^2 +
d_1^2 d_2 d_4 \, v_4^2,
\]
where $P_1 = \sum_j h_{1j} v_j$, $Q_1 = (h_{11}h_{22} - h_{12}^2) v_2 + (h_{11}h_{23} -
h_{12}h_{13}) v_3 + (h_{11}h_{24} - h_{12}h_{14}) v_4$, and $Q_2$ is the third linear form
(\code{sym4\_Q2}). With every $d_k > 0$ the right side is nonnegative and the factor on the
left is positive.
\end{proof}

[404] Level 1+AB
The cascade for the full check runs on $H$. Step one: $H_{11} > 0$ and the $5 \times 5$
matrix $S^{(6)}_{ij} = H_{11} H_{ij} - H_{1i} H_{1j}$, $2 \le i, j \le 6$, gives a nonnegative
form. Step two: $S^{(6)}_{22} > 0$ and the $4 \times 4$ matrix
$S^{(5)}_{ij} = S^{(6)}_{22} S^{(6)}_{ij} - S^{(6)}_{2i} S^{(6)}_{2j}$, $3 \le i, j \le 6$,
gives a nonnegative form. Step three: the four leading minors of $S^{(5)}$ are positive. The
real predicates are \code{sym5\_pd\_interior}, \code{sym6\_pd\_interior} and the witness
\code{q1ab\_g12345\_minors\_witness}, which implies \code{column\_contractive\_q1ab} and
then \code{PSD9} (\code{q1ab\_g12345\_minors\_witness\_implies\_psd9}).

[405] Level 1+AB
\begin{definition}[What the integer check computes]\label{def:chsh-g12345}
The supplied moments arrive as bucket pairs: $\gamma_k = N_{g_k}/D_{g_k}$ with
$N_{g_k} = \mathrm{same}_{g_k} - \mathrm{diff}_{g_k}$ and
$D_{g_k} = \mathrm{same}_{g_k} + \mathrm{diff}_{g_k}$. Watch the letters: for these five
buckets the source swaps them, so here $N$ is the difference and $D$ the count, the reverse
of item A. I keep the source's letters so the names below match the files. Let
$K = (N_{00} N_{01} N_{10} N_{11} D_{g_1} D_{g_2} D_{g_3} D_{g_4} D_{g_5})^2$
(\code{g12345\_COMMON\_Z}, kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v). The check computes the 21 integers
$K H_{ij}$ (\code{cleared\_g12345\_H11\_Z} to \code{cleared\_g12345\_H66\_Z}), the 15
integers of $S^{(6)}$ at scale $K^2$ and the 10 integers of $S^{(5)}$ at scale $K^4$, each by
\code{schur\_step\_Z}, $h_{11} h_{ij} - h_{1i} h_{1j}$ on integers. The
decider \code{q1ab\_g12345\_check\_z\_kernel} is the conjunction of: $N_{xy} > 0$ for the four
pairs; $D_{g_k} > 0$ for $k = 1..5$; $-D_{g_k} < N_{g_k} < D_{g_k}$ for $k = 1..5$; and six
strict positivities, namely $K H_{11} > 0$, $K^2 S^{(6)}_{22} > 0$, and the four leading
minors of the integer $S^{(5)}$. The full check
\code{q1ab\_g12345\_full\_integer\_check\_kernel} is \code{column\_contractive\_check\_witness} of item
E and this decider.
\end{definition}

[406] Level 1+AB
The bridge lemmas, for example \code{cleared\_g12345\_H11\_Z\_bridge}
(QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v), prove that each cleared integer, read as a real, equals the
corresponding real quantity times the stated positive power of $K$. A $k \times k$ leading
minor of a matrix scaled by $c > 0$ scales by $c^k$. So every integer positivity holds exactly
when the real one does.

[407] Level 1+AB
\begin{theorem}[The full 1+AB check implies PSD]\label{thm:chsh-g12345}
Let the counts make every $N_{xy}$ and every $D_{g_k}$ positive, and let
\code{q1ab\_g12345\_check\_z\_kernel} pass on them. Then the matrix of
Definition~\ref{def:chsh-nine} at the four correlators and $\gamma_k = N_{g_k}/D_{g_k}$ is
symmetric and \code{PSD9}. This is \code{q1ab\_g12345\_caller\_witness\_z\_abs\_sound} followed by
\code{q1ab\_g12345\_minors\_witness\_implies\_psd9}
(kernel/quantum/QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v).
\end{theorem}

[408] Level 1+AB
\begin{proof}
The bucket conjuncts make every denominator positive, the bridges carry the six integer
positivities to the real cascade, and Lemma~\ref{lem:chsh-schur-step} twice plus
Lemma~\ref{lem:chsh-sylvester} give $R \ge 0$, hence \code{PSD9} by Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-nine-iff}.
\end{proof}

[409] Level 1+AB
\begin{remark}[Interior only]\label{rem:chsh-interior}
Every test in the cascade is strict, so a passing check certifies that $H$ is positive
definite, which is stronger than semidefinite. A point where $H$ is PSD but singular cannot pass, and
neither can a moment with $|\gamma_k| = 1$. For example, the tally of item E with correlators
$(3/5, 4/5, 4/5, -3/5)$ has $H_{11} = 1 - 9/25 - 16/25 = 0$, so the full 1+AB check fails on it
for every choice of $\gamma$, though the level-1 check of item E accepts it
(\code{nec\_e\_singular\_full\_fails} and \code{nec\_e\_singular\_level1\_passes},
NecEChshEquality.v). The source proves
soundness only; no converse is stated.
\end{remark}

[410] Level 1+AB
A worked example of the cascade, in exact rational arithmetic, checked by computation as
\code{nec\_e\_worked\_cascade\_passes}, \code{nec\_e\_worked\_cascade\_fails\_at\_zero} and
\code{nec\_e\_worked\_level1\_passes} (NecEChshEquality.v). Take the correlators $(0.6, 0.6, 0.6, -0.6)$ and
$\gamma = (0, 0, 0, 0, -1/2)$; as buckets, $\gamma_1$ to $\gamma_4$ are $(1,1)$, giving
$0/2$, and $\gamma_5$ is $(1,3)$, giving $-2/4$, both inside the strict bounds. Then
$H_{11} = H_{22} = 7/25$, $H_{12} = 0$, and every entry $H_{1j}$, $H_{2j}$ with $j \ge 3$ is
zero. So $S^{(6)}_{22} = (7/25)^2 = 49/625$, and $S^{(5)}$ is a positive multiple of the lower
right block of $H$:
\[
S^{(5)} = \frac{343}{781250}
\begin{pmatrix}
32 & -18 & -18 & -7 \\
-18 & 32 & 7 & 18 \\
-18 & 7 & 32 & 18 \\
-7 & 18 & 18 & 32
\end{pmatrix},
\]
where $50 H_{33} = 32$, $50 H_{34} = 50 H_{35} = -18$, $50 H_{36} = 50(-1/2 + 0.36) = -7$,
$50 H_{45} = 50(1/2 - 0.36) = 7$, and $50 H_{46} = 50 H_{56} = 18$. The leading minors of the
integer matrix are $32$, $700$, $15000$ and $140625$, all positive. With
$H_{11} = 7/25 > 0$ and $S^{(6)}_{22} > 0$, all six tests pass, and with item E's $A = B = 175$,
$C = 0$ the full check passes. At $\gamma_5 = 0$ instead, the same block becomes
$I_4 - \mathbf e \mathbf e^{\mathsf T}$ with $\mathbf e = (0.6, 0.6, 0.6, -0.6)$, whose third
leading minor is negative ($\tfrac{1}{25^3}$ times $-1250$), and the check fails.

[411] Level 1+AB
That last fact is general. The zero-moment check
\code{column\_contractive\_check\_q1ab\_kernel} (kernel/quantum/CHSHColumnCheck.v) takes no
$\gamma$ buckets; it is the level-1 check together with
\code{sum\_E\_sq\_check\_witness}, the integer form of
$E_{00}^2 + E_{01}^2 + E_{10}^2 + E_{11}^2 \le 1$ with denominators cleared by
$(N_{00} N_{01} N_{10} N_{11})^2$.

[412] Level 1+AB
\begin{theorem}[The zero-moment check sits inside the classical bound]\label{thm:chsh-gzero}
If \code{column\_contractive\_check\_q1ab} $w$ = \code{true} (QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v, the same
function), then the four correlators of $w$ satisfy
$E_{00}^2 + E_{01}^2 + E_{10}^2 + E_{11}^2 \le 1$
(\code{q1ab\_check\_at\_gzero\_forces\_unit\_ball}) and $|S| \le 2$
(\code{q1ab\_check\_at\_gzero\_implies\_classical\_bound}).
\end{theorem}

[413] Level 1+AB
\begin{proof}
At $\gamma = 0$ the residual splits into the level-1 form in $(v_{B_0}, v_{B_1})$ and
$|\mathbf v|^2 - \langle \mathbf e, \mathbf v \rangle^2$ in
$\mathbf v = (v_{00}, v_{01}, v_{10}, v_{11})$, where
$\mathbf e = (E_{00}, E_{01}, E_{10}, E_{11})$ (\code{q1ab\_residual\_g\_zero\_decomp}). The
check gives column contractivity at $\gamma = 0$
(\code{column\_contractive\_check\_q1ab\_sound\_at\_g\_zero}). Test at $v_{B_0} = v_{B_1} = 0$ and
$\mathbf v = \mathbf e$: with $r = |\mathbf e|^2 \ge 0$, the residual is $r - r^2 \ge 0$, so
$r \le 1$. Then Lemma~\ref{lem:chsh-sos} gives $S^2 \le 4 r \le 4$.
\end{proof}

[414] Level 1+AB
So the zero-moment check certifies a strict part of the classical region: no point
above $|S| = 2$, and none of the sixteen deterministic points either, since each has
$|\mathbf e|^2 = 4$. A point above 2 needs supplied moments.
\code{q1ab\_certifies\_a\_superclassical\_chsh\_point} (QuantumPartitionPSD\_\allowbreak{}1AB.v) proves \code{PSD9}
at correlators $(3/5, 3/5, 3/5, -3/5)$, $\gamma = (0,0,0,0,-1/2)$, with $S = 12/5$, through
the $\gamma_5$ slice: its real condition \code{q1ab\_g5\_caller\_witness} asks $-1 < \gamma_5 <
1$ and
\[
\begin{aligned}
&(1 - \gamma_5)\bigl[(E_{00} + E_{11})^2 + (E_{01} - E_{10})^2\bigr] \\
&\quad + (1 + \gamma_5)\bigl[(E_{00} - E_{11})^2 + (E_{01} + E_{10})^2\bigr]
\le 2(1 - \gamma_5^2).
\end{aligned}
\]
At this point the left side is $\tfrac32 \cdot 0 + \tfrac12 \cdot (\tfrac{36}{25} +
\tfrac{36}{25}) = \tfrac{36}{25} = 1.44$ and the right side is $2 \cdot \tfrac34 = 1.5$. The
integer version, \code{q1ab\_g5\_kernel\_check\_accepts\_superclassical}, runs the $\gamma_5$
check on the counters $(4,1), (4,1), (4,1), (1,4)$ and the bucket $(1,3)$ and returns
\code{true} by computation.

[415] Level 1+AB
Each of the four 1+AB checks (CHSHColumnCheck.v) contains
\code{column\_contractive\_check\_witness} as a conjunct, so a passing 1+AB check also passes the
level-1 check and inherits $S^2 \le 8$ from item E. That is a composition I did by reading
the definitions; the source has no single theorem stating it.

[416] The elliptope completion
\begin{definition}[Completion and membership]\label{def:chsh-elliptope}
\code{completed\_npa} (kernel/quantum/ElliptopeCompletion.v) is the record of item D with the
within-party entries set to free reals: $x$ in the $(A_0, A_1)$ cells and $y$ in the
$(B_0, B_1)$ cells, marginals still zero. The correlators are elliptope realizable
(\code{elliptope\_realizable}) when some $x, y$ make the completed matrix symmetric and PSD.
\end{definition}

[417] The elliptope completion
Its quadratic form at $v = (v_0, \ldots, v_4)$ is
$\sum_k v_k^2 + 2x v_1 v_2 + 2y v_3 v_4 + 2E_{00} v_1 v_3 + 2E_{01} v_1 v_4 + 2E_{10} v_2 v_3
+ 2E_{11} v_2 v_4$ (\code{completed\_quad\_expand}). The pinned matrix is the case $x = y = 0$,
so PSD of $M(E)$ implies membership: \code{zero\_marginal\_implies\_elliptope}.

[418] The elliptope completion
\begin{theorem}[Deterministic plans are inside, \code{deterministic\_strategy\_elliptope}]\label{thm:chsh-ell-det}
(ElliptopeCompletion.v) If $a_0^2 = a_1^2 = b_0^2 = b_1^2 = 1$, then
$(a_0 b_0, a_0 b_1, a_1 b_0, a_1 b_1)$ is elliptope realizable, with $x = a_0 a_1$ and
$y = b_0 b_1$.
\end{theorem}

[419] The elliptope completion
The all-ones plan is the instance \code{classical\_tightness\_witness\_elliptope}, and the
classical mixture $(1, 0, 1, 0)$ is \code{turing\_point\_elliptope}, at $x = 1$, $y = 0$, with
form $v_0^2 + (v_1 + v_2 + v_3)^2 + v_4^2$.

[420] The elliptope completion
\begin{theorem}[Convexity and finite mixtures]\label{thm:chsh-ell-convex}
For $0 \le \lambda \le 1$, if $E$ and $F$ are elliptope realizable, so is
$\lambda E + (1 - \lambda) F$ (\code{elliptope\_convex}, ElliptopeCompletion.v). For finitely
many members with nonnegative weights summing to at most 1, the weighted sum is realizable
(\code{elliptope\_finite\_mixture}). Every finite mixture of deterministic plans is realizable
(\code{lhv\_mixture\_elliptope}).
\end{theorem}

[421] The elliptope completion
\begin{proof}
The form is affine in $(E, x, y)$ for each fixed $v$. Take the completion
$(\lambda x_1 + (1-\lambda) x_2, \lambda y_1 + (1-\lambda) y_2)$; the form at the mixture is
the same mixture of two nonnegative forms. The finite version is induction on the number of
members, with the shortfall in weight absorbed by the zero point (\code{elliptope\_zero}).
The last statement combines this with Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-ell-det}.
\end{proof}

[422] The elliptope completion
\begin{theorem}[Tsirelson's bound holds on the completion set, \code{elliptope\_tsirelson}]\label{thm:chsh-ell-tsirelson}
(ElliptopeCompletion.v) If $E$ is elliptope realizable then $S^2 \le 8$.
\end{theorem}

[423] The elliptope completion
\begin{proof}
For a symmetric PSD matrix the bilinear form obeys Cauchy-Schwarz,
$\mathcal B(u, w)^2 \le \mathcal Q(u)\, \mathcal Q(w)$ (\code{psd\_cauchy\_schwarz}), because
$\mathcal Q(u + t w) = \mathcal Q(u) + 2t\,\mathcal B(u,w) + t^2 \mathcal Q(w) \ge 0$ for all
$t$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:chsh-discriminant} applies. Take $u_1 = e_{A_0}$ and
$w_+ = e_{B_0} + e_{B_1}$: $\mathcal B(u_1, w_+) = E_{00} + E_{01}$, $\mathcal Q(u_1) = 1$,
$\mathcal Q(w_+) = 2 + 2y$. Take $u_2 = e_{A_1}$ and $w_- = e_{B_0} - e_{B_1}$:
$\mathcal B(u_2, w_-) = E_{10} - E_{11}$, $\mathcal Q(w_-) = 2 - 2y$. So
$c_1^2 \le 2 + 2y$ and $c_2^2 \le 2 - 2y$ with $c_1 = E_{00} + E_{01}$,
$c_2 = E_{10} - E_{11}$, and both budgets are nonnegative. Then
$c_1^2 c_2^2 \le (2 + 2y)(2 - 2y) = 4 - 4y^2 \le 4$, so $c_1 c_2 \le 2$, and
$S^2 = (c_1 + c_2)^2 = c_1^2 + 2 c_1 c_2 + c_2^2 \le (2 + 2y) + 4 + (2 - 2y) = 8$.
\end{proof}

[424] The elliptope completion
\begin{corollary}[The PR box is outside, \code{pr\_box\_not\_elliptope}]\label{cor:chsh-ell-pr}
(ElliptopeCompletion.v) $(1, 1, 1, -1)$ is not elliptope realizable, since its
$S^2 = 16 > 8$.
\end{corollary}

[425] The elliptope completion
\begin{theorem}[Strictly beyond the classical bound, \code{beyond\_classical\_elliptope}]\label{thm:chsh-ell-beyond}
(ElliptopeCompletion.v) $(3/5, 3/5, 3/5, -3/5)$ is elliptope realizable and has
$S = 12/5 > 2$.
\end{theorem}

[426] The elliptope completion
The gate (kernel/quantum/ElliptopeGate.v) is an integer decider with a soundness proof. Its
completion entries arrive as bucket pairs, and an empty bucket is read as $0/1$ (\code{bden}),
so unlike item E it does not require every pair to be sampled.

[427] The elliptope completion
\begin{definition}[The two branches]\label{def:chsh-gate}
\code{elliptope\_pd\_check} (ElliptopeGate.v): with the completion supplied as buckets and all
entries scaled to integers by a positive common factor $K$, the four leading minors of
the $4 \times 4$ block on $A_0, A_1, B_0, B_1$ are strictly positive. \code{elliptope\_ldl\_check}: with the completion and a rational certificate supplied (six subdiagonal entries over
a common positive denominator, four pivots $d_k \ge 0$ over another), the ten entry equations
of $M = L D L^{\mathsf T}$ hold in cross-multiplied integer arithmetic (\code{ldl\_equations}). \code{elliptope\_check\_full} is their disjunction.
\end{definition}

[428] The elliptope completion
\begin{theorem}[Soundness of the gate, \code{elliptope\_check\_full\_sound}]\label{thm:chsh-gate-sound}
(ElliptopeGate.v) If \code{elliptope\_check\_full} returns \code{true} on a counter record, any
completion buckets and any certificate, then the four correlators of the record are
elliptope realizable. And \code{elliptope\_full\_gate\_never\_accepts\_pr\_box}: on the PR box
tally the gate returns \code{false} for every choice of the remaining arguments.
\end{theorem}

[429] The elliptope completion
\begin{proof}
The first branch reduces to Lemma~\ref{lem:chsh-sylvester} through the same fraction-free identity
(\code{elliptope\_pd\_check\_sound}). The second is a weighted sum of squares: given the ten
equations, the form is $\sum_k d_k P_k^2$ with $d_k \ge 0$ (\code{elliptope\_ldl\_check\_sound}). The PR statement is soundness followed by Corollary~\ref{cor:chsh-ell-pr}.
\end{proof}

[430] The elliptope completion
Computed examples, each closed by \code{reflexivity}: \code{gate\_accepts\_all\_ones}, the
all-ones plan, through the second branch at $x = y = 1$; \code{gate\_accepts\_turing\_point};
\code{gate\_accepts\_beyond\_classical}, the $12/5$ point, through the first branch at
$x = y = 0$; \code{gate\_accepts\_pythagorean\_boundary}, the point $(3/5, 4/5, 4/5, -3/5)$,
through the second branch with pivots $(1, 1, 0, 0)$.

[431] What is not proved
The arcsine boundary. For two settings and two outcomes per side, Tsirelson, Landau (1988) and
Masanes (2003) give theorems characterizing the correlators reachable by quantum measurements:
$|\arcsin E_{00} + \arcsin E_{01} + \arcsin E_{10} - \arcsin E_{11}| \le \pi$ and its sign
variants. The characterization is checked in ArcsineBoundary.v as an if and only if with the
completion set (\code{am\_arcsine}) and, through Tsirelson's representation, with the quantum
set (\code{am\_quantum\_arcsine}). The point $(3/5, 4/5, 4/5, -3/5)$ is on the boundary:
$\arcsin \tfrac35 + \arcsin \tfrac45 = \tfrac{\pi}{2}$ by the 3-4-5 triangle, so the sum is $\pi$
(\code{am\_pythagorean\_boundary}); Tsirelson's point is on it too (\code{am\_tsirelson\_boundary}).
The necessary direction holds in any dimension, for any expectation on an algebra of
observables in which Alice's commute with Bob's (\code{ta\_arcsine}, TsirelsonAlgebraic.v).

[432] What is not proved
Equality with the quantum set. That the completion set of item G is exactly the set of
quantum correlators is Tsirelson's representation theorem, and it is proved in finite dimension:
\code{tr\_representation} for real amplitudes and \code{tr\_representation\_complex} for complex
ones (TsirelsonRepresentation.v), with two eight-level systems enough for every point. That PSD of the level-1 moment matrix is necessary for
quantum behaviour is proved for strategies in finite dimension, with real amplitudes
(\code{qs\_npa\_psd}, QuantumStrategies.v) and with complex ones (\code{qc\_npa\_psd},
QuantumStrategiesComplex.v), and in any dimension, marginals included, for any expectation on
an algebra of observables (\code{ta\_npa\_psd}, TsirelsonAlgebraic.v).

[433] What is not proved
Achievability. Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-tight} is a point of $\mathbb{R}^4$. A state and
measurements that reach it are built in QuantumStrategies.v: the two-qubit state
$(|00\rangle + |11\rangle)/\sqrt 2$ with $A_0 = Z$, $A_1 = X$, $B_0 = (Z + X)/\sqrt 2$ and
$B_1 = (Z - X)/\sqrt 2$ has correlators $1/\sqrt 2$, $1/\sqrt 2$, $1/\sqrt 2$, $-1/\sqrt 2$ and
$S = 2\sqrt 2$ (\code{qs\_tsirelson\_reached}), and every strategy in finite dimension has
$S^2 \le 8$, with real amplitudes (\code{qs\_tsirelson}) and with complex ones
(\code{qc\_tsirelson}, QuantumStrategiesComplex.v). No experiment stands behind either.

[434] What is not proved
Completeness of the checks. The 1+AB cascade and the elliptope gate are proved sound, and neither
has a proved converse. The level-1 integer check is the exception: on the small machine the same
seven integer facts hold exactly when every pair was sampled and the pinned matrix is positive
semidefinite (\code{small\_chsh\_check\_iff}, Theorem~\ref{thm:chsh-exact}), so that check is
complete for what it tests. The 1+AB
cascade is strict and misses boundary points. The comment in ElliptopeGate.v that the second
branch accepts every point with a rational PSD completion is a mathematical remark about
$LDL^{\mathsf T}$ factorizations, and the file has no theorem for it; nor is there a theorem that the
four 1+AB checks accept nested sets.

[435] Prior work
Tsirelson's 1980 paper gives the bound $2\sqrt 2$ for quantum correlations, and his theorem
says that quantum two-outcome correlators are inner products of real unit vectors. Landau (1988)
and Masanes (2003, arXiv quant-ph/0309137) each give a condition that, by their proofs,
characterizes the quantum correlators exactly for two settings and two outcomes. Wehner (2006,
arXiv quant-ph/0510076) gets Tsirelson bounds, CHSH among them, from a semidefinite program on
the $4 \times 4$ Gram matrix of those vectors. Navascués,
Pironio and Acín (2007, arXiv quant-ph/0607119; 2008, arXiv 0803.4290) built the hierarchy of
moment-matrix tests; their 2008 paper applies a Schur complement to the level-1 matrix of this
scenario, recovers the arcsine condition, and discusses the 1+AB level. I went back to each of
those papers for these points, and that is what I found in them. The results are theirs. Two of
them are proved again here in Coq, in finite dimension: Tsirelson's representation
(TsirelsonRepresentation.v) and the arcsine characterization (ArcsineBoundary.v); the rest of
their proofs are not redone. Formal proofs of Tsirelson's
bound already exist, by Echenim and Mhalla in Isabelle/HOL (arXiv 2306.12535) and in Lean's
mathlib (tsirelson\_\allowbreak{}inequality in Mathlib.Algebra.Star.CHSH), and exact rational
sum-of-squares certificates go back at least to Peyrl and Parrilo (2008). So the
moment-matrix route to $2\sqrt 2$ is standard. What is specific here is narrower: an integer
certificate computed from outcome counts, and a mechanized chain from ``the counts pass'' to PSD
of the pinned matrix and on to $S^2 \le 8$. I did not find that chain elsewhere, and I make no
claim beyond it.
On the host machine, the same chain is earned: a raised flag from a clean start
stands on a passing check of the packed counts, and the check passes exactly when the pinned matrix
is positive semidefinite and every pair was sampled (Section~\ref{sec:chsh},
Theorems~\ref{thm:chsh-exact} and~\ref{thm:chsh-earned}). The converse direction of the integer check,
integers from PSD, is checked there too, and there too I make no claim beyond the chain.

[436] Numerals and syntax as data
The three syntax constructors, operating on already encoded children, are
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{mkvar}&=\lambda n.\lambda v.\lambda a.\lambda l.v\,n,\\
\mathrm{mkapp}&=\lambda p.\lambda q.\lambda v.\lambda a.\lambda l.a\,p\,q,\\
\mathrm{mklam}&=\lambda p.\lambda v.\lambda a.\lambda l.l\,p.
\end{aligned}
\]
Each builder is a term of the language $L$ of Definition~\ref{def:u12-L}.
For a variable of index $n$, an application and an abstraction, respectively, define
their codes by the following resulting values:
\[
\begin{aligned}
\ulcorner\#n\urcorner&=\lambda v.\lambda a.\lambda l.v\,\overline n,\\
\ulcorner p\,q\urcorner&=\lambda v.\lambda a.\lambda l.a\,\ulcorner p\urcorner\,\ulcorner q\urcorner,\\
\ulcorner\lambda p\urcorner&=\lambda v.\lambda a.\lambda l.l\,\ulcorner p\urcorner.
\end{aligned}
\]
In particular, $\mathrm{mkvar}\,\overline n$, $\mathrm{mkapp}\,\ulcorner p\urcorner\,\ulcorner q\urcorner$ and
$\mathrm{mklam}\,\ulcorner p\urcorner$ reduce to these three codes. A code is closed even when
the term it describes has a free variable: the index of that variable is stored
as a numeral. It is data inside the code, and the code itself has no free variable.

[437] Quoting terms
The $\mathrm{mkapp}$ used in Section~\ref{sec:diagonal} is this builder. With $Q$ and that builder written out, its self-referential
term has every piece on
the page. The companion names are \code{mk\_app\_enc}, \code{rec\_spec}, \code{Qn\_spec} and \code{Q\_spec}
(LRecursion.v); the construction above is the argument those names identify.
